Um, no, I wouldn't say I'm fatigued. My energy levels are actually... they're pretty normal, I think. I mean, in my line of work, you kind of have to stay on your toes and be ready for anything, so I'm used to having pretty good stamina.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I know I just spent all that time telling you how I can't seem to get going with my hobbies or chores, and I really hope it doesn't sound like I'm contradicting myself now—I want to make sure I'm giving you the most accurate picture possible.

Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... no, no major traumas or anything like that. I mean, my job can be a little demanding sometimes, being in protective services and all, but it's nothing I can't handle. I've been doing it a long time, so I'm used to the day-to-day stress of it. 

There hasn't been any big event or tragedy in my life recently, thankfully. Everything at home is fine, and work is... well, it's just work. I really don't think there's anything external that's causing this. I've always been pretty resilient, you know? I try not to let things get to me too much. 

I hope that's okay to say. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information so we can figure this out. I'm doing my best to be a good patient for you. Is there anything else you need to know about that?

I know there are things I need to do, I see them right in front of me, but I just can't seem to make myself start. \n\nI hope I'm not disappointing you by saying all that. I just want to be completely open with you so I'm doing my part right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that even when you do manage to start an activity, it doesn’t feel as rewarding or satisfying as it used to?\n\nPatient: Um, well...
